The pointer arithmetic on unk64 is the issue here: it's a typed `struct actor *`, so adding 0x36 scales by the struct size (0x10C) rather than adding bytes as the original s32 did. That's a semantic change, and it matches the c22 "arith" problem that c22b's arith_s32.py was meant to fix.

The arith fix from c22b is the pattern to apply here: a typed `->unk64 + K` (and `->unkD4 + K`) needs an `(s32)` cast so the offset adds bytes, not struct-sized steps. c22 said the engine had already respelled these sites to actor paths, but this one wasn't, so I'll check arith_s32.py to reuse its approach.

Splitting the dry runs into four chunks per actor, sprite and model parent, plus one for fx and two extra lines for the duplicate-parent windows, puts about fifteen running in parallel, which the earlier sixteen-way run suggests memory can handle. Each chunk should take roughly thirty minutes, since the target walk dominates the time. After the write I also need to confirm that the untypeable count never goes up, reverting any key that pushes it.

The update is in. Here's what it changes:
- **No more false "stopped with Ctrl+C" stops.** Sometimes a coder finished just as its expert dropped off Claude Code's agent list. PA3 then took that as a Ctrl+C, paused the task and asked you about it. That's likely what happened with T17's second "Ctrl+C" this morning. It won't happen any more. A real Ctrl+C still pauses the task, as before.
- **You see more of what coders do.** The main session now shows the line an expert or coder writes before it waits on a long command, such as "The gate suite is running now". Files that a script changes now show as edit cards too, like edits made with the edit tools.
- **Switching models with `/model` mid-answer is safe.** The switch waits until I'm free, so nothing is lost and the cache isn't wasted.

The mod's own files changed too, so no task starts until Claude Code restarts. T17 stays paused either way, and it goes on only when you say so.

Project Architect 3.32.20, a hotfix (No false stops, and you see what coders do), is installed.

**To load it: /exit, then claude --continue (not /clear)**

  - **First engine refusal (`unk4` vs `unk6`):** once any key on a line has a `member:` token, the engine stops mapping common fields onto the actor's leaves (restruct.py:4986), and 44 of the 61 keys have one. Second attempt, using the W2 recipe: I respelled the keys' common-field casts to the actor leaves by hand first (1102 sites).
  - **Second engine refusal (`blkDC` vs `unkE0`):** the engine only sees top-level gaps, so it can't place fields in the nested extC. I placed the keys' extC fields by hand into each token's variant (33 sites). That added 5 new extC variants and grew kSC04_002_801871EC and kSC06_018_801932EC.

  **unkD4 union:** a rename probe found the fixpoint in one round: 500 lines in 191 files, not the ~228 the brief estimated. The field is now `union { struct Unkstruct_801202A0 *p; struct { u16 lo; u16 hi; } u; } unkD4`, and all 500 uses are spelled `.p`. I also gave unkD8 `struct { u16 lo; u16 hi; } u`. The 6 pointee-u16 rows are 18 reads, now spelled `unk64->unkD4.u.lo`, `unkD4.u.hi` and `unkD8.u.lo`. A negative control (changing one read's `.lo` to `.hi`) changed the asm, so the check does see these reads.

  **Ledger:** I appended this batch's note to the 800B5CB8 row. No new rows were needed (only variants were added).

- **Recommendation:** add a real `RenderHead` struct as the first member of both the sprite record (`Unkstruct_800B5BD8`) and the model record (`Unkstruct_8001C9D0`). Add an `h` leaf to the actor/fx +0x20 pointer union, and type these readers as `RenderHead`. This is the SOTN common-header pattern, and it is a member of real types, so not a fragment. It needs one applier batch (c38) in T17. The cost is respelling the sprite and model head-field accesses across the fleet, about 4,000+ cast-form sites plus typed uses, expected to stay byte-identical.
